# Support matrix

The aim is one behaviour in every language: each implementation of Bead plays the same conformance files and is proven against the others in the interop. A difference stays on this page only until it closes. The Rust side plays part of them while its steps and stages land ([0044](decisions/0044-rust-one-owner-per-router-traits-no-unsafe-two-parameters-no-warning.md)): each part ahead is a row of the [differences](#differences), of kind open, which `spec/check.py` holds by its `AHEAD`, and which each step deletes as it lands.

Every cell is read from the tests. A cell that names a test file is supported, and that file, with the `describe` (TypeScript), the test class or method (C#) or the test function or trial (Rust) named beside it, proves it. "Not tested" means no test holds it, whatever the code does; "not available" means the implementation has no such part. TypeScript test paths are under `implementations/typescript/src/`, C# test paths under `implementations/dotnet/tests/`, Rust test paths under `implementations/rust/crates/`, but those written from the repository's root. The sections of `spec/spec.md` say what each feature is, and those of its companion `spec/debugging.md`, cited as "debugging §", what a side traces, filters and counts beside the wire, and those of `spec/transcoding.md`, cited as "transcoding §", what a side's gateway answers over HTTP; [the decisions](decisions/README.md) say why it is so.

The TypeScript side's unit tests run under Node alone, in one Playwright project and no browser: the Web Worker transport is tested over a `MessageChannel`, and the clients over Node's `WebSocket`, `ws` and `fetch`. One client runs in a browser: the HTTP client, bundled into a page Chromium loads from another origin than the interop service's HTTP server it calls on each side, and into a page whose own server answers its connect with a redirect, by `implementations/typescript/browser/__tests__/cors-page.test.ts` under `playwright.browser.config.ts` (`yarn test:browser`).

Over a broker, both sides do the same, and what that costs is stated in its records
([0038](decisions/0038-over-a-broker-a-connection-is-placed-once-at-its-open-named-by-both-its-ends.md)):
calls spread across a name's participants by connection, not by call; a participant added takes no
connection already placed, and one stopped ends every connection of its own; and each participant
needs a broker identity of its own. Ahead, the same on both sides: a connection's age and a
graceful drain, for every long-lived transport at once, a WebSocket's and a broker's, as HTTP/2's
`GOAWAY` drains one ([RFC 9113 §6.8](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9113#section-6.8)) and
gRPC's `MAX_CONNECTION_AGE` re-spreads them; an acceptor that proves itself to its opener; a
publisher's identity a broker vouches for, standing for an `ELSEWHERE`, RabbitMQ's validated
`user-id` its way over AMQP 1.0
([0043](decisions/0043-over-amqp-1-0-a-participant-is-one-connection-whose-exclusive-inbox-ends-with-it-and-a-parcel-one-message-of-one-profile-each-counted-to-its-byte.md));
a relay that carries a subscription, which a page across a broker needs
([0019](decisions/0019-a-relay-terminates-each-leg-and-routes-nothing-past-it.md)); and, over MQTT 5,
an Authentication Method, MQTT 5's enhanced authentication
([§4.12](https://docs.oasis-open.org/mqtt/mqtt/v5.0/os/mqtt-v5.0-os.html)), and EMQX played beside
Mosquitto
([0041](decisions/0041-over-mqtt-5-a-participant-is-one-clean-link-and-a-parcel-one-publish-each-counted-to-its-byte.md)).

MQTT 5 is a broker's first binding, both sides alike, and what it costs is stated in its record
([0041](decisions/0041-over-mqtt-5-a-participant-is-one-clean-link-and-a-parcel-one-publish-each-counted-to-its-byte.md)): every case of the in-memory broker that a real broker can play plays
against the Mosquitto 2.1.2 `tools/mqtt-broker.mjs` starts, which each side's suite skips, saying
so, where no broker is given, and which CI's job `mqtt` requires. A deployment's access control
grants a name on its own topic, and one that denies a server's participant leaves its clients'
connects to time out; a participant's link carries its broker's Receive Maximum a round trip; a
link taken over is fenced, and its participant connects no more until it is started again; and
Mosquitto 2.1.2 counts a Remaining Length as one byte toward a subscriber and as none from a
publisher, which Bead's own count never reaches. Its WebSocket listener takes no fragmented message,
so every side sends each as one frame, a row of [Leveled down](#leveled-down).

AMQP 1.0 on RabbitMQ 4 is a broker's second binding, both sides alike, and what it costs is stated
in its record
([0043](decisions/0043-over-amqp-1-0-a-participant-is-one-connection-whose-exclusive-inbox-ends-with-it-and-a-parcel-one-message-of-one-profile-each-counted-to-its-byte.md)):
every case of the in-memory broker that a real broker can play but the will's and the takeover's
plays against the RabbitMQ 4.4.0 `tools/amqp-broker.mjs` starts, which each side's suite skips,
saying so, where no broker is given, and which CI's job `amqp` requires. The C# side's bytes are
AMQPNetLite 2.5.4's, which takes no message already written, held to the profile by
`amqpMessages`, which fails on the day an upgrade writes another encoding; the TypeScript side
writes the profile itself and hands rhea 3.0.5 its bytes. A participant is one connection, its
inbox exclusive to it; a name its broker denies ends its own session alone; no receiver names a
max-message-size; and the publisher's identity a broker vouches for is ahead, RabbitMQ's
validated `user-id` its way over AMQP 1.0. Each part that parts it from MQTT 5 is a row of
Differences, its kind the standard's or the platform's.

## Interop

Each client is played against the other implementation's server of the interop service (`spec/interop-blueprint.json` and `spec/interop-service.json`), over each transport both serve, and each side's generated HTTP client against the gateway the other side's interop server serves beside it, of `spec/transcoding.proto`'s service.

| TypeScript | C#         | WebSocket              |    19 | `implementations/typescript/src/client/__tests__/interop.test.ts` (`the C# server of the interop service`, `over WebSocket`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| TypeScript | C#         | HTTP/1.1               |    18 | the same file (`over HTTP`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| C#         | TypeScript | WebSocket              |    17 | `implementations/dotnet/tests/Bead.Interop.Tests/InteropTests.cs` (`InteropTests`, each theory over `WebSocket`, and the transfer fact)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| C#         | TypeScript | HTTP/1.1               |    16 | the same file (each theory over `HTTP`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| TypeScript | C#         | MQTT 5                 |    24 | `implementations/typescript/src/client/__tests__/interop.test.ts` (`the C# server of the interop service`, `over MQTT 5`): its cases over WebSocket, and a name served by two in one group, a deliberate copy, a part, a will and a link lost and its connections reopened                                                                                                                                                                    |
| C#         | TypeScript | MQTT 5                 |    22 | `implementations/dotnet/tests/Bead.Interop.Tests/InteropTests.cs` (each theory over `MQTT`, and the same five a broker alone has)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| TypeScript | C#         | AMQP 1.0               |    23 | `implementations/typescript/src/client/__tests__/interop.test.ts` (`the C# server of the interop service`, `over AMQP 1.0`): its cases over WebSocket, and a name served by two in one group, a deliberate copy, a part and a link lost and its connections reopened                                                                                                                                                                          |
| C#         | TypeScript | AMQP 1.0               |    21 | `implementations/dotnet/tests/Bead.Interop.Tests/InteropTests.cs` (each theory over `AMQP`, and the same four a broker alone has over it)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| TypeScript | C#         | HTTP/JSON, its gateway |     5 | `implementations/typescript/src/client/__tests__/interop.test.ts` (`the C# server of the interop service`: `calls the other gateway with protobuf JSON and every native encoding`, `reads the other gateway problems and presents its authorization`, `reads the other gateway result notifications and ending failure`, `reads the other gateway own route size and busy refusals`, `compares the gateways OpenAPI documents byte for byte`) |
| C#         | TypeScript | HTTP/JSON, its gateway |     5 | `implementations/dotnet/tests/Bead.Interop.Tests/HttpInteropTests.cs` (`HttpInteropTests`: `Calls_the_other_gateway_with_protobuf_JSON_and_every_native_encoding`, `Reads_the_other_gateway_problems_and_presents_its_authorization`, `Reads_the_other_gateway_result_notifications_and_ending_failure`, `Reads_the_other_gateway_own_route_size_and_busy_refusals`, `Compares_the_gateways_OpenAPI_documents_byte_for_byte`)                 |
| either     | either     | HTTP/2                 |     0 | not tested                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| either     | either     | broker                 |     0 | not available: the in-memory broker joins no two processes; over a real broker, MQTT 5's rows and AMQP 1.0's                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| either     | either     | Web Worker             |     0 | not available (platform)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Rust       | either     | any                    |     0 | ahead: `Rust's interop`, with its transports over HTTP and WebSocket                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

The cases are the same on both sides: a payload echoed, and a value outside ASCII echoed equal with its members in another order, a string of the length asked, the application's own failure, an unknown procedure and service, an admission that refuses, a resolver that throws, a payload that is not JSON, a request its rules refuse, answered `PAYLOAD_INVALID` with its violations, and one they take, a call cancelled by its signal and one past its timeout (each cancelled at the server too), a command the server asks, an unpaced subscription and a paced one, a session opened and ended, a call presenting the session it learned, a `JWT` presented by default and run in its credential's session, an `ELSEWHERE` carried in each `POST`'s `Authorization` over HTTP and in its upgrade's over a WebSocket, durations held as section 1.4 holds them, and, over WebSocket alone, 3 MiB each way as transfers. The TypeScript side counts more tests than cases, as it splits the echo from the bytes, and the refusals, into tests of their own where the C# side gathers them in theories. Over the other side's gateway, each generated HTTP client calls with protobuf's JSON and every native encoding, reads its problems and presents its authorization, reads a stream's result, its notifications and the failure that ends it, and the gateway's own refusals of a route, a size and a busy stream, and compares the two gateways' OpenAPI documents byte for byte: five tests a side, each twin named by the same sentence.

The conversations and the transports' cases hold a server to them, and over a broker a client's
transport too, by the client role of the transports' file. A client's behaviour over HTTP and a
WebSocket is held to the other side by the interop alone, until the transports' file has a client
part for them, which is ahead, by a decision of its own, but for what it holds of a duration it is
given, which the durations' file holds.

What no entry plays stays with each side's twin tests, which name each unit they hold by its own
number, `spec 15.10` or `debugging 4.2`, in a test's title or a comment just above its declaration,
where `spec/units.txt` or `spec/debugging-units.txt` gives it `twins`, and `spec/check.py` holds
that a test of each side does:

- what a writer refuses and what a client alone does, which the conversations, playing a server,
  cannot reach: what a writer never writes (spec 4.10), `router/shared/__tests__/header.test.ts`
  (`writeHeader`, its `refuses …` tests) and
  `Bead.Core.Tests/EnvelopeCodecHeaderTests.Refuses_to_write_a_header_and_writes_nothing`; a
  failure's detail as a writer cuts it (spec 7.4), `envelope.test.ts` (`writeFailure`) and
  `EnvelopeCodecTests` (`Cuts_the_detail_at_the_limit_at_a_character_s_boundary`,
  `Writes_a_lone_surrogate_as_U_FFFD`); a code's block and a code no name gives,
  kept as its number (spec 8.1), `constants.test.ts` (`ProtocolCode`) and `errors.test.ts`
  (`FailureError`), and `FailureExceptionTests.Keeps_a_protocol_code_no_name_has_as_a_number`;
  the reservation made with the cancel (spec 10.4), `router-requester.test.ts`
  (`cancel a call still waiting once: rejected, told, and its call id reserved on its connection alone`)
  and `RouterCoreTests.Requester.cs`; a subscription's end with its connection, and a notification
  under none (spec 11.7, its third item), and the client's credits (spec 11.11),
  `router/client/__tests__/router-client.test.ts` (`notifications`, `paced subscriptions`) and
  `RouterClientTests`; the limits' defaults (spec 12.2), `router-limits.test.ts` (`its limits`) and
  `RouterLimitsTests.Holds_by_the_defaults_both_ends_set_alike`; a late failure and a late
  notification at a client (spec 12.9, its second and third rows), `router-requester.test.ts`
  (`take an answer too late in silence while its call id is reserved, then report it`) and
  `router-client.test.ts`, and `RouterCoreTests.Requester.cs`
  (`Drops_a_late_answer_while_its_call_id_is_reserved_then_reports_it`) and `RouterClientTests`;
  section 12.11's bytes, `transfer.test.ts` (`lays the spec’s 20 MiB out byte by byte: …`) and
  `TransferTests.Lays_the_spec_s_20_MiB_out_byte_by_byte_…`; a relayed answer leg A's transport
  did not take, and one past leg A's limit (spec 14.5),
  `router/server/__tests__/router-server.test.ts` (`createRouterServer, relaying`:
  `hands leg A’s transport the relayed result once, answers nothing after it refuses it, and reports why with leg A’s connection`,
  `answers a relayed result past leg A’s limit PROCEDURE_FAILED with no detail, and reports PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE with leg A’s connection`)
  and `RouterServerTests.Relay.cs`
  (`Hands_leg_A_s_transport_the_relayed_result_once_answers_nothing_after_it_refuses_it_and_reports_why_with_leg_A_s_connection`,
  `Answers_a_relayed_result_past_leg_A_s_limit_PROCEDURE_FAILED_with_no_detail_and_reports_PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE_with_leg_A_s_connection`);
  and a client's listens (spec 15.10), `transport/client/__tests__/http-transport-client.test.ts` and
  `Bead.Transport.Http.Tests/HttpTransportClientTests`
  (`says at connect that it listens, then listens: …`,
  `listens again from where it was when a listen is lost …`);
- the interop service's own readers, by `spec/interop-service.json`'s `verifiers`: its
  `ELSEWHERE` verifier's verdicts and what `endSessionsOf` takes and refuses,
  `router/server/__tests__/conversations.test.ts`
  (`the interop service’s ELSEWHERE verifier holds, refuses and fails each value as its words say, at its time on its server’s clock`,
  `the interop service’s endSessionsOf takes an issuer alone, a string, and throws for anything else`)
  and `Bead.Core.Tests/InteropCredentialsTests` and `InteropHandlersTests`, each twin named by the
  same sentence; and `undefined`, which no JSON value is and only a TypeScript caller passes,
  refused on the TypeScript side alone;
- a destination that is a router the relay calls through, with its two legs' limits and the session
  every requester presenting none or `OPAQUE` shares behind it where its admission takes leg A's
  session (spec 14.3 and 14.7):
  `router/server/__tests__/relay.test.ts` and `Bead.Core.Tests/RelayTests`, until the interop plays a
  relay across languages; and its leg B refused or lost there over HTTP (spec 14.5, its fourth and
  fifth rows), whose responses the twins script, until the transports' file has a client part over
  HTTP and a WebSocket:
  `server/__tests__/http-server.test.ts`
  (`relay a call whose leg B goes over HTTP: refused there, nothing tried; lost there, it may have run`)
  and `Bead.Transport.Http.Tests/HttpServerTests.Relay_a_call_whose_leg_B_goes_over_HTTP_refused_there_nothing_tried_lost_there_it_may_have_run`;
- the turn a request presenting a `JWT` or an `ELSEWHERE` resolves in, at a side and at a relay that
  forwards it (spec 9.2, 14.3), which a turn of each side's scheduler gives and no peer observes:
  `router/shared/__tests__/router-core.test.ts` (`a request's credential`,
  `of a JWT or an ELSEWHERE resolves in a later turn, whether its resolution waits or not, its call id held meanwhile`,
  `of a JWT or an ELSEWHERE its handler forwards resolves to none in a later turn all the same, a request presenting none delivered behind it taken first`)
  and `Bead.Core.Tests/RouterCoreTests.Sessions.cs`
  (`A_credential_of_a_JWT_or_an_ELSEWHERE_resolves_in_a_later_turn_whether_its_resolution_waits_or_not_its_call_id_held_meanwhile`,
  `A_credential_of_a_JWT_or_an_ELSEWHERE_its_handler_forwards_resolves_to_none_in_a_later_turn_all_the_same_a_request_presenting_none_delivered_behind_it_taken_first`);
- the result of a request that opens a subscription to a procedure that notifies, too large for one
  envelope, which subscribes nothing (spec 11.2, 11.6): `router/server/__tests__/router-server.test.ts`

  the bytes of body under way past `bodyBytesBudget` (spec 15.12),

  (`hands each envelope on whole, once and in order, in a later turn than its send`,

  (`reconnects through the name it serves, a pair drawn anew`,
  `starts once its broker holds each name it serves, fails its start on a name refused, and stops once it let each go`,

  `keeps a Server Keep Alive shorter than its own: a PINGREQ once nothing went for that long (spec 15.26)`,

  `tells its link taken over on a DISCONNECT of 0x8E, and lost on any other end (spec 15.26)`,
  `tells its link lost once, and nothing of a lost link reaches the next: no message, no acknowledgement, no PUBACK’s reason, no quota (spec 15.26)`,
  `refuses what is asked while its link is down, and leaves no library to reconnect, subscribe again or hold a publication (spec 15.26)`,
  `refuses a group once its CONNACK says Shared Subscription Available 0 or its SUBACK 0x9E (spec 15.25)`,
  `subscribes at QoS 1, No Local 0, Retain As Published 0 and Retain Handling 2, a group as its shared subscription, held once its SUBACK grants QoS 1 and refused otherwise (spec 15.27)`,
  `publishes at QoS 1 and RETAIN 0, a life in whole seconds rounded up where one is asked, and no other property (spec 15.28)`,
  `holds its QoS 1 publications to its CONNACK’s Receive Maximum, the next one going once a PUBACK came, and delays no other packet for them (spec 15.28)`,
  `reads a PUBACK of 0x10 as no subscriber, under 0x80 as taken, and 0x80 or more as refused (spec 15.28)`,
  `refuses a publication past its CONNACK’s Maximum Packet Size before it goes (spec 15.28)`,
  `hands each message on before its PUBACK, which goes once it is settled, and once (spec 15.29)`,
  `drops a message whose RETAIN is 1, acknowledging it (spec 15.29)`,
  `closes its link, no DISCONNECT, on a packet past its own Maximum Packet Size (spec 15.29)`,
  `announces as its receive bound the largest payload of a message to its inbox within its Maximum Packet Size, a life counted (spec 15.30)`,
  `gives up a start, a subscription or a publication its signal aborts, a publication that waits on its quota leaving it unsent, and retires its link where what it asked may have gone`,

  `carries a link over a WebSocket of subprotocol mqtt (spec 15.25)` and
  `sends each WebSocket message to its broker as one binary frame, a burst past 64 KiB among them (spec 15.25)`,

- what of AMQP 1.0 no case reaches (spec 15.32 to 15.38), its adapter against a scripted AMQP

  (`opens a fresh connection each start, its container-id its id, SASL PLAIN as its id or EXTERNAL under a certificate, its idle-time-out its own, and leaves no library to reconnect or recover it (spec 15.33)`,
  `declares its inbox exclusive, classic and not durable through management on its own connection, binds it to its root’s exchange, and is up once its receiver attached (spec 15.33)`,
  `fails its start on a declaration answered other than 200 or 201, naming it, and while another connection of its id holds the inbox, its participant linking once it is let go (spec 15.33)`,
  `fails its start and closes its connection when its management’s link is detached, its session ended or its connection lost between its inbox’s declaration and its binding, and leaves no queue of its id behind (spec 15.33)`,
  `tells its link lost once on the end of its connection, or of its inbox’s session or receiver, closes its connection, and lets nothing of a lost link reach the next (spec 15.33)`,
  `attaches a held name’s receiver again once, on a fresh session, when its broker ends it, telling no loss, and tells its link lost when that is refused or the name is lost again, never attaching a name it lets go (spec 15.33)`,
  `refuses each publication a sending session held as it ends, keeps its link, and begins another for the next (spec 15.33)`,
  `subscribes a name on a session of its own, unsettled, first, no max-message-size, held once its ATTACH named its source (spec 15.34)`,
  `refuses a name whose ATTACH names no source and is detached, whose session its broker ends, or past its broker’s channel-max, naming it, and keeps every other (spec 15.34)`,
  `grants its inbox’s receiver linkCredit as it attaches and one more as each message is settled, never as it arrives (spec 15.34)`,
  `grants a name’s receiver a credit of 1, so that of the OPENs its broker holds for a member with room for one it hands on one, the others left in the name’s queue, none refused (spec 15.34)`,
  `lets a name go once its DETACH came, and its inbox once management deleted it, telling no loss (spec 15.34)`,
  `lets a name go within one connectTimeout, what it handed on settled and its DETACH answered, and tells its link lost past it (spec 15.34, 15.37)`,
  `waits, subscribing a name under way, for its ATTACH to name its source, and lets a name whose subscription an unsubscription met go once it attached, never held (spec 15.34)`,
  `hands on and credits what comes to its inbox while its deletion is under way, and keeps its inbox where its deletion is refused or not answered within connectTimeout (spec 15.34, 15.37)`,
  `publishes each parcel as one message of format 0 to the anonymous terminus, unsettled, its to the topic’s exchange address, a life its header’s ttl in milliseconds rounded up where one is asked, and nothing else (spec 15.35)`,
  `holds its publications to its sender’s credit, the next going as credit comes, in their order, and delays no receiver for them (spec 15.35)`,
  `reads accepted as taken, released as no subscriber, and rejected or any other outcome as refused (spec 15.35)`,
  `refuses a publication past its sender’s max-message-size before it goes (spec 15.35)`,
  `hands each message on before its accepted, which goes once it is settled, on the receiver it came on, and once, never released or modified (spec 15.36)`,
  `hands on a message whose body is other than one Data section, or whose payload passes its receive bound, as no parcel, its link kept (spec 15.36)`,
  `announces as its receive bound the largest payload of a message to its inbox within its maximumMessageSize, and refuses as it is built a maximumMessageSize outside 1 to 4,294,967,295 and a linkCredit outside 1 to 2,147,483,647 (spec 15.37)`,
  `gives up a start, a subscription or a publication its signal aborts: a start’s connection closed, a name’s session ended, a publication that waits on credit left unsent`,
  `stops by detaching its receivers and closing its connection with no error, its inbox gone with it (spec 15.33)`,
  `refuses a departure, offering no will (spec 15.32)`,
  `is secure over amqps, and over amqp to the loopback alone`,
  `carries a link through RabbitMQ’s topic exchange, a name’s queue consumed by two in turn (spec 15.32)`),
  the third, fourth, seventh, ninth, twenty-first, twenty-fourth and twenty-seventh against the

  which skip where no broker is given; its management, `amqp-management.test.ts` and
  `AmqpManagementTests`
  (`pairs its sender and receiver under one name on a session of its own, one after the other, and matches each answer by its correlation-id`,
  `fails each request under way when its link is detached, its session ends or its connection is lost, and matches no answer of an ended pair to a later request`),
  each twin named by the same sentence;
- the trace of calls and of envelopes (debugging §4, §5), which no file plays: the twins its row
  of the Features names;
- HTTP/2 and TLS, which both servers serve: open, as the transports' cases play HTTP/1.1 in clear;
- keep-alive, pipelining and a connection used again, which one socket per exchange leaves out;
- the transport's contract with its router, its events, its handles and its gauges, which each side's
  transport tests hold;
- a transport's listener that throws, every other listener still called and the throw reported
  through its transport's `error` with its connection (spec 15.1; decision 0023), which no step of
  the transports' cases installs: `transport/server/__tests__/transport-server.test.ts`
  (`calls each listener though one throws, reporting the throw with its connection`) and
  `Bead.Core.Tests/ListenerGuardTests`; `http-transport-server.test.ts` and
  `Bead.Transport.Http.Tests/HttpTransportServerTests` (`draws a connection whose connection
  listener throws, …`, `hands on a request whose message listener throws, …`, `stops though a close
  listener throws, …`, `closes a connection idle past its timeout though a close listener throws,
  …`); `ws-transport-server.test.ts` and `WebSocketTransportServerTests` (`keeps a connection whose
  connection listener throws, …`, `hears the next message after a message listener throws, …`);
  `transport/client/__tests__/ws-transport-client.test.ts` and `WebSocketTransportClientTests`
  (`hears the next message after a message listener throws, the throw reported`;
  `reconnects though a reconnect listener throws, the throw reported, the next listener told`);
  `http-transport-client.test.ts` and `HttpTransportClientTests` (`hears the rest of a batch when a
  listener throws, the throw reported`; `reconnects though a reconnect listener throws, …`), each twin
  named by the same sentence; and the server's transport, as the server gives it, carrying `error`
  and `refusal` alike: `server/__tests__/server.test.ts` and `Bead.Core.Tests/ServerTests`
  (`hears its transport's error and refusal through the server's transport`);
- what a server's stop answers what arrives during it, and the order its refusals keep inside it (spec
  15.6), which no step can send into (spec 15.2.8, 15.3, 15.6 rows 6 and 13, 15.7; decision 0023):
  `transport/server/__tests__/http-transport-server.test.ts`
  and `HttpTransportServerTests` (`refuses a connect that comes as it stops, busy, told when to come
  back, drawing nothing`; `answers the 404 marked connection-unknown an exchange that comes as it
  stops, its connection not yet closed, nothing handed on`); `ws-transport-server.test.ts` and
  `WebSocketTransportServerTests` (`refuses an upgrade that comes as it stops, 503 busy, told when to
  come back`; `closes going away a connection whose accept a stop crossed`), each twin named by the
  same sentence;
- a connection's handle, drawn as it is taken and given once, two sockets from one address and port
  two connections (spec 15.1; decision 0025), which no step opens: `ws-transport-server.test.ts` and
  `WebSocketTransportServerTests` (`names each connection by its peer’s port and the rank it came at,
  …`, `closes each of two sockets from one address and port on its own, …`);
  `server/__tests__/ws-server.test.ts` and `WebSocketServerTests` (`keeps two sockets from one
  address and port, to two of its addresses, as two connections, …`), on Linux, the C# one on Windows
  too; `http-transport-server.test.ts`, `ws-transport-server.test.ts`, `HttpTransportServerTests` and
  `WebSocketTransportServerTests` (`names a client that comes over IPv4 to a dual-stack listener by
  its IPv4 address`), each twin named by the same sentence;
- a request's authority over HTTP/2, its `:authority` before a `host`, and its stream reset when it
  is no host (spec 15.12; decision 0024), which no step sends, the steps being HTTP/1.x heads:
  `http-transport-server.test.ts` and `HttpTransportServerTests` (`names its host over HTTP/2 by its
  :authority, …`, `resets over HTTP/2 the stream of a request whose :authority is no host, …`); and a
  `hosts` or an `origins` entry refused as a server is built (spec 15.12; decision 0024), which no
  step builds: `transport/server/__tests__/hosts-and-origins.test.ts` and
  `Bead.Core.Tests/HostsAndOriginsTests` (`refuses a hosts entry with a port, an empty one, one that
  is no host, and * beside another`, `refuses an origins entry that is * or null, or not an origin
  as RFC 6454 serializes it`), `http-transport-server.test.ts`, `ws-transport-server.test.ts`,
  `HttpTransportServerTests` and `WebSocketTransportServerTests` (`refuses as it is built a hosts
  entry with a port, and an origins entry that is * or null`), each twin named by the same sentence;
- the own origin a server takes by its request's authority only when it takes that host by name
  (spec 15.12; decision 0024), which the cases under `*` play on the wire and no step reaches
  function by function: `hosts-and-origins.test.ts` and `HostsAndOriginsTests` (`ownAuthorityOf
  gives the authority of a host taken by name, every server’s or one listed, and none of one only *
  takes`), `ws-handshake.test.ts` and `WebSocketHandshakeTests` (`takes whatever its hosts the hosts
  every server takes and any page of its own by a host it takes by name, …`), each twin named by the
  same sentence;
- the CORS answer as a browser reads it, its preflight over HTTP/2, and the listed origin's
  predicate (spec 15.12; decision 0027), which no step reaches, the steps being HTTP/1.x heads sent
  by no browser: `http-transport-server.test.ts` and `HttpTransportServerTests` (`answers a
  preflight over HTTP/2 as over HTTP/1.1`), `hosts-and-origins.test.ts` and `HostsAndOriginsTests`
  (`isListedOrigin takes an Origin that origins names exactly, …`, `IsListedOrigin_…`), each twin
  named by the same sentence; and a page of another origin in Chromium, the HTTP client bundled
  for it, connecting, calling, listening and disconnecting against both servers, and listening
  through a stream of batches on one preflight:
  `implementations/typescript/browser/__tests__/cors-page.test.ts`, under `playwright.browser.config.ts`, in CI's .NET job.
  Firefox's and WebKit's reading of the answer and of `*` and a keepalive `DELETE` as its page goes
  stay with decision 0027's measurement, in Chromium 153, Firefox 155 and WebKit 26.6, and so do
  their preflight caches (decision 0029): Chromium 153 and WebKit 26.6 keep one entry per origin
  and URL, replaced by the last answer, and match `*`; Firefox 155 merges the names its answers
  list, stores `*` as a token nothing matches, and preflights its first two exchanges at a URL
  whatever their methods, measured; whether a Firefox entry outlives its host's DNS TTL
  (`CORSCacheEntry::CheckDNSCache`) is unmeasured, a hostname of short TTL its check, the Playwright
  project holding Chromium's;
- a listen told held and told ended as one takes another's place (spec 15.1, 15.9; decision 0012):
  the listen that takes the place is told held before the one it replaces is told ended, so a
  server's count of the listens a connection holds never falls to none between them, which no step
  hears, a transports' case reading the transport's `listens` gauge and never the order its listens
  are told in:
  `http-transport-server.test.ts` and `HttpTransportServerTests` (`tells a listen held, once past
  its refusals, and its end while its connection stands: one refused tells nothing and restarts
  nothing, and a second and the first it replaces each tell theirs`), each twin named by the same
  sentence;
- a listen's counts in its fields and their grammar, past what a step sends (spec 15.8, 15.9, 15.12;
  decision 0029): each side's RFC 9651 Item parser over httpwg's `structured-field-tests`,
  `transport/shared/__tests__/structured-fields.test.ts` and `Bead.Core.Tests/StructuredFieldsTests`
  (`parses an Item as RFC 9651 does: …`); the client listening at its connection's URL alone and
  reading the 201's `bead-listen` and a batch's `bead-sequence` as Items,
  `http-transport-client.test.ts` and `HttpTransportClientTests` (`listens at its connection’s URL

  `http-server-fields.test.ts` and `HttpTransportServerTests` (`reads a listen’s counts from

  `http-transport-server.test.ts` and `HttpTransportServerTests` (`resets over HTTP/2 the stream of
  a request with a field whose value starts or ends with a space or a tab, PROTOCOL_ERROR, …`), each

- the address a server listens on, `localhost` by default, both loopbacks, or an IP address written

  runner starting on `127.0.0.1`: `http-transport-server.test.ts`,

  (`isIPAddress takes a bare IP address …`, `IsIpAddress_…`), each twin named by the same

- a client naming the refusal it reads, and connecting by a name to a server on one loopback family

- the words of a debug filter's refusal and of a declaration's, each side's own, as a C side reports
  a code (debugging §6.7, §7.3): `router/shared/__tests__/debug-filter.test.ts`
  (`refuses a filter in sentences that name the word, or the value, as it was written, quoted as JSON`)
  and `Bead.Core.Tests/DebugFilterTests.Refuses_a_filter_in_sentences_that_name_the_word_or_the_value_as_it_was_written_quoted_as_JSON`;
  `metrics-registry.test.ts`
  (`refuses a declaration as it is made, in sentences that name its address, its names and its reason, declaring nothing`)
  and `MetricsRegistryTests.Refuses_a_declaration_as_it_is_made_in_sentences_that_name_its_address_its_names_and_its_reason_declaring_nothing`;
- the words of a codec's refusal and of a declaration's off section 16, each side's own, and what a
  value that does not write becomes (spec 17.2): `router/shared/__tests__/payloads.test.ts`,
  `identity.test.ts`, `service-manager.test.ts`, `service-requester.test.ts` and
  `service-resolver.test.ts`, and `Bead.Core.Tests/PayloadsTests`, `IdentityTests` and
  `ServiceManagerTests`, as the features above name them;
- a client's own exits that no conversation reaches — each refusal made before its request goes, its
  caller's cancel, `error` — and a registry given to two routers:
  `router/client/__tests__/router-client.test.ts` (`metrics`),
  `router/shared/__tests__/router-core.test.ts` and `router-requester.test.ts` (`its metrics`) and
  `router/server/__tests__/router-server.test.ts` (`createRouterServer, its metrics`), and
  `Bead.Core.Tests/RouterClientTests.Metrics.cs`, `RouterCoreTests.Metrics.cs`,
  `RouterCoreTests.Requester.cs` and `RouterServerTests.Metrics.cs`; and the C# side's threads,
  `RouterClientTests.Counts_exactly_under_calls_made_at_once_from_many_threads`.

The Rust side's twins carry the other sides' sentences in snake case, each under a comment naming
its unit, `// spec 15.13`, in a module's `tests.rs` beside it or a crate's `tests/`: spec 15.13's in
`bead/src/memory/tests.rs`,
`hands_each_envelope_on_whole_once_and_in_order_in_a_later_turn_than_its_send`,
`carries_the_credential_a_request_presents_beside_it_and_none_toward_its_client`,
`hands_its_router_the_address_and_the_port_its_client_gave_as_it_connected`,
`draws_a_handle_of_type_memory_for_each_connect_and_gives_none_twice`,
`tells_the_other_end_of_a_close_in_a_later_turn_the_closer_s_clean`,
`closes_each_connection_as_it_stops_and_takes_no_connect_after` and
`carries_a_transfer_under_its_routers_credit`; spec 15.14's in `bead/src/joined/tests.rs`,
`hands_on_each_transport_s_events_with_the_handle_it_drew`,
`asks_each_connection_s_flags_of_the_transport_that_drew_it_and_sends_and_closes_through_it`,
`refuses_to_join_two_transports_of_one_type`,
`stops_every_transport_it_joined_and_sums_their_gauges` and
`serves_one_service_offered_once_to_a_client_of_each_transport`; and what of a broker no case
reaches, in `bead/src/broker/tests.rs`, by the other sides' sentences above. Those of a unit ahead
for it, spec 15.10 and 15.12 and the transcoding's, come with the stage that brings it. Beside
them, its own: an operation that takes effect as it is made, its future only waiting,
`sends_each_call_in_the_order_it_is_made_whatever_order_it_is_awaited_in`,
`a_call_s_future_dropped_unpolled_still_sends_its_call` and
`a_call_a_listener_makes_within_a_turn_goes_as_that_turn_ends_before_any_job_queued_after_it`; a
router that lives while its transport holds it,
`keeps_its_connection_when_the_application_drops_its_last_handle`; and a router built outside a
runtime, or on one without its time driver, refused, `a_router_built_outside_a_runtime_is_refused`
and `a_router_on_a_runtime_without_its_time_driver_is_refused`, each in `bead/src/router/tests.rs`;
and a resolver's own failure and any other error,
`answers_a_resolver_s_own_failure_as_it_is_and_any_other_error_procedure_failed_with_no_detail_and_reported`,
in `bead/src/router/tests.rs` too.

## An example's halves

`examples/hello` is a project written as a consumer writes one
([0022](decisions/0022-a-proto-becomes-one-checked-blueprint-and-each-language-is-generated-from-it.md)):
its TypeScript and C# halves, each built with its messages generated by beadc and by its platform's
own generator. The C# half's external build compiles Grpc.Tools' messages, from its own protoc,
under beadc's service code; the TypeScript half's runs protoc-gen-es under `buf generate` at the pin
beadc runs, so it proves the project's own generation and `messagesOut`'s import, not another
generator's output. Each client calls each server over WebSocket and writes
[`transcript.txt`](../examples/hello/transcript.txt), then each server's gateway answers what a
person asks it with curl over HTTP/1.1, through the in-memory transport joined to its WebSocket one:
CI's `examples` job, through `examples/hello/pairs.mjs`. What a consumer of the TypeScript runtime's
sources must set, which the C# half never meets, is a row of the [differences](#differences).

| Client          | Server         | Messages                | Transport                    | Pairs | Test                                                                          |
| --------------- | -------------- | ----------------------- | ---------------------------- | ----: | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| TypeScript, C#  | TypeScript, C# | beadc's, the platform's | WebSocket                    |    16 | `examples/hello/pairs.mjs`                                                    |
| curl's requests | TypeScript, C# | beadc's, the platform's | HTTP/1.1, the gateway        |    16 | `examples/hello/pairs.mjs`, the gateway's lines of `transcript.txt`           |
| either          | either         | either                  | HTTP/1.1, the HTTP transport |     0 | not run: the interop holds HTTP; the example serves Bead over WebSocket alone |

## beadc's wrappers

A project runs beadc's image through two wrappers it commits, `beadc.sh` under bash and `beadc.ps1`
under PowerShell
([0022](decisions/0022-a-proto-becomes-one-checked-blueprint-and-each-language-is-generated-from-it.md)).
They are one contract: each case of `builder/src/__tests__/wrappers.test.ts` runs both, `beadc.ps1`
by `pwsh -File` and inside a session, against a stand-in engine that records each call, and holds
them to one engine call, one output and one exit, so a difference between them is a red test. Its
cases run on every leg of CI's `builder` job, on Linux, amd64 and arm64, and on Windows. What one
shell cannot do is a row of a kind the [differences](#differences) define, with the test that shows
it.

| Behaviour                                                | Kind     | `beadc.sh`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `beadc.ps1`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------------------------------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| The shell that runs it                                   | platform | bash 3.2 or later; `sh`, where it is not bash, refused, exit 3: `wrappers.test.ts` (`each wrapper refuses the shell it is not written for, exit 3`)                                                                                                                                                              | PowerShell 7.6 or later; Windows PowerShell 5.1, which drops an empty argument and a quote from a native command's arguments and splits the mount's path at a space, refused, exit 3, where its execution policy lets the file load: the same test, under `Bypass`; 7.4 and 7.5, out of support from 10 Nov 2026, refused, exit 3, by the same check, which no runner's pwsh, 7.6.6, reaches                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| One engine call, one output and one exit for one call    | —        | `wrappers.test.ts` (`each wrapper makes the same engine calls, prints the same bytes and exits alike: …`), its cases on Linux and on Windows, each host's links among them, junctions on Windows                                                                                                                 | the same test, each case by `pwsh -File` and inside a session                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| An execution policy that refuses the file, on Windows    | platform | —                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | PowerShell's exit 1 and its message, before a line of the wrapper runs: Windows PowerShell's `Restricted`, the Windows client's default, a group policy's `AllSigned`, `RemoteSigned` on a file marked as downloaded; `pwsh -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File ./beadc.ps1` runs it: `wrappers.test.ts` (`an execution policy that refuses beadc.ps1 stops PowerShell before its first line, exit 1`), `pwsh` under `AllSigned` and `powershell` under `Restricted`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| A redirect inside a session on Windows                   | platform | the engine's bytes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | the engine's characters, UTF-8, each line ended CRLF by PowerShell's `>` and `2>`; by `pwsh -File`, the engine's bytes: `wrappers.test.ts` (`in a session, beadc.ps1 passes the engine's UTF-8 whole, and a > on Windows writes its lines CRLF`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| A bare `--`                                              | platform | passed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | passed by `pwsh -File`; taken by PowerShell inside a session, and `'--'`, quoted, passed: `wrappers.test.ts` (`in a session, a bare -- does not reach the engine, and a quoted '--' does`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| A `-name:value` token                                    | platform | passed; `--against-ref`, the wrappers' own, takes `<ref>` after it or after `=`, and `--against-ref:<ref>` is refused, exit 2, as `pwsh -File` would split it: `wrappers.test.ts` (`--against-ref:<ref> is refused, exit 2, where pwsh -File splits it into --against-ref <ref>`)                                | split at its colon by `pwsh -File`, `--except:A` and `-o:f` alike; inside a session, `-o:f` reaches it as `f`, its name taken by PowerShell, and `--except:A` whole; quoted, passed whole. beadc's own forms, `--except=A` and `--except A`, pass alike every way: `wrappers.test.ts` (`a -name:value token reaches the engine split by pwsh -File and cut in a session; quoted, whole`). `--against-ref`, the wrappers' own, takes `<ref>` after it or after `=`; `--against-ref:<ref>`, whole inside a session, is refused, exit 2, and split by `pwsh -File`, runs as `--against-ref <ref>`: the same file (`--against-ref:<ref> is refused, exit 2, where pwsh -File splits it into --against-ref <ref>`) |
| An archive piped into the wrapper, for `--against -`     | platform | passed to the engine, which `-i` gives its standard input: `wrappers.test.ts` (`in a session, an archive piped into beadc.ps1 is refused, exit 2, where bash and pwsh -File pass it on`)                                                                                                                         | passed by `pwsh -File`; inside a session, refused, exit 2, as PowerShell hands a script what is piped to it as objects, never as the engine's standard input: `--against-ref` takes the ref instead, the same test                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `--against-ref`'s run inside a session                   | platform | —                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | the engine's bytes on the process's own streams, past the session's `>` and `2>`: the archive is piped by two processes of .NET's, since PowerShell's pipe into a native command drops its exit when it closes its standard input before the end, as beadc refusing its arguments does; by `pwsh -File`, the engine's bytes, as every run: `wrappers.test.ts` (`in a session, --against-ref's run writes the process's streams, past the session's > and 2>`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| A folder whose name holds a backslash, on Linux or macOS | platform | runs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | refused, exit 3, naming `beadc.sh`: PowerShell reads a backslash as a separator on every platform, and cannot stand in such a folder: `wrappers.test.ts` (`beadc.ps1 refuses a folder whose name holds a backslash, exit 3, where beadc.sh runs`), on Linux                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| A host or an engine no leg runs                          | open     | macOS, WSL, Podman (its `--init` needs `catatonit` on the host), rootless Docker, an SELinux host, a mapped drive on Windows: not tested on a host, the stand-in alone; an interrupt's 130 under the wrappers' flags on Linux: `wrappers.test.ts` (`an interrupt ends a run under the wrappers' flags with 130`) | the same; and its exit after a Ctrl-C, where pwsh stops the script it interrupts, not tested                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

## Differences

A difference is of one of three kinds. **Platform**: a platform imposes it, and it stays while the platform does. **Standard**: a binding's standard parts it from another binding's, every side alike, and it stays while the standards do. **Open**: nothing imposes it, and closing it is work to do.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | Over HTTP/1.1 and at a WebSocket's address, the parsers strip the padding (RFC 9110 §5.5) and both sides answer a control character 400 unmarked (spec 15.12). Neither request is taken past what a request without the field would be: a dropped `bead-after` leaves a listen refused 400, a dropped `Authorization` an anonymous request, a dropped `Origin` a request that names none. Kestrel ends the session on NUL or a lone octet that is no UTF-8 in `:path`, as on a field that is no UTF-8, where Node resets the stream; neither takes the request. |

## Leveled down

A leveling is no difference, since every side behaves alike: it is a place where Bead does less than a standard, or its own wire, allows, because one platform cannot do more and every side keeps to what it can ([0004](decisions/0004-one-behaviour-in-every-language.md)). Each is kept here with what would lift it, so that it can be lifted, on every side at once, once the platform can.

| Leveling                                                                             | The standard                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Every side                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | What forces it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | What would lift it                                                                                                                                                                    | TypeScript
